Minimum 60 seconds\n            throw new ConfigurationException($\"{nameof(BatchWindowSeconds)} must be between 60 and 3600 seconds.\");\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Evidence 5: Batch Orchestrator with S3 Persistence\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/src/module_64.txt\n// Lines: 66-118\npublic async Task<KpiBatchResult> ProcessBatchAsync(\n    IEnumerable<KpiRequest> requests,\n    CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n{\n    if (requests == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(requests));\n\n    var requestList = requests.ToList();  // Full materialization\n    _logger.LogInformation(\"Processing {Count} KPI requests\", requestList.Count);\n\n    var throttler = new SemaphoreSlim(_options.DegreeOfParallelism);\n    var resultCollector = new ConcurrentBag<KpiComputationResult>();\n    var failures = new ConcurrentBag<KpiComputationFailure>();\n\n    var tasks = requestList.Select(async request =>\n    {\n        await throttler.WaitAsync(cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n        try\n        {\n            await ExecuteWithRetryAsync(request, cancellationToken, async () =>\n            {\n                var strategy = _strategyRegistry.Resolve(request.KpiName);\n                // ...\n                var result = await strategy.ComputeAsync(request, cancellationToken)\n                    .ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                await PersistToLakeAsync(result, cancellationToken)  // S3 write per result\n                    .ConfigureAwait(false);\n                // ...\n            }).ConfigureAwait(false);\n        }\n```\n\n### Evidence 6: Timer-Based Flushing (Batch Assumption)\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/src/module_43.txt\n// Lines: 97-113\npublic FocusTimeRatioTransformation(\n    ILogger<FocusTimeRatioTransformation>? logger = null,\n    int maxBufferSize                          = 5_000)  // Large buffer assumption\n{\n    _logger        = logger;\n    _maxBufferSize = maxBufferSize;\n    _flushTimer    = new Timer(_ => FlushBufferAsync().ConfigureAwait(false), null,\n                               _flushInterval, _flushInterval);  // 30-second flush interval\n}\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Core Components Identification\n\n**Stream Processing Pipeline:**\nThe stream processing pipeline is triggered by Kinesis Data Streams and designed for low-latency, per-record processing:\n\n| Module | Class | Purpose |\n|--------|-------|---------|\n| module_72.txt | `KinesisStreamProcessor` | Primary Lambda entry-point with lightweight cold-start |\n| module_80.txt | `StreamTransformationCoordinator` | High-throughput Kinesis processor with bounded parallelism |\n| module_68.txt | `TransformationLambda` | Kinesis-triggered Lambda with EventBridge fan-out |\n| module_22.txt | `CalendarMetricLambda` | Domain-specific stream processor |\n\n**Batch Processing Pipeline:**\nThe batch processing pipeline is designed for high-volume, scheduled runs (hourly/daily) with heavy initialization:\n\n| Module | Class | Purpose |\n|--------|-------|---------|\n| module_64.txt | `KpiComputationOrchestrator` | Main batch orchestrator with S3 persistence |\n| module_86.txt | `TransformationOrchestrator` | Glue/Step-Functions compatible orchestrator |\n| module_34.txt | `KpiComputationEngine` | Strategy-based KPI engine for batch jobs |\n| module_35.txt | `TransformationDispatcher` | Reflection-based strategy dispatcher |\n| module_10.txt | `KpiTransformationEngine` | Batch transformation with S3 parquet output |\n| module_65.txt | `EventTransformationPipeline` | TPL Dataflow-based batch pipeline |\n| module_60.txt | `KpiStrategyRegistry` | Attribute-based strategy discovery |\n\n### 2. Bottleneck Analysis for Micro-Batching\n\nThe current batch processing architecture has several critical bottlenecks that make it unsuitable for the 30-60 second micro-batch requirement with a 90-second SLA:\n\n#### A. High Startup/Cold-Start Overhead\n\n**Problem:** The batch modules rely on reflection-based strategy discovery that scans all loaded assemblies on every instantiation.\n\n```csharp\n// module_86.txt, module_60.txt, module_35.txt all use this pattern:\nvar strategies = AppDomain.CurrentDomain.GetAssemblies()\n    .SelectMany(a => a.GetTypes())\n    .Where(t => typeof(ITransformationStrategy).IsAssignableFrom(t))\n    .Select(Activator.CreateInstance)...\n```\n\n**Impact:** Assembly scanning and type activation takes 500ms-2000ms depending on deployment size. This alone could consume 2-3% of the 90-second SLA on every invocation.\n\n#### B. TPL Dataflow Pipeline Initialization\n\n**Problem:** The `EventTransformationPipeline` (module_65.txt) creates three TPL Dataflow blocks with bounded capacities and links them together on every instantiation:\n- `BufferBlock` with 1,024 capacity\n- `TransformManyBlock` with configurable parallelism\n- `ActionBlock` for sink operations\n\n**Impact:** Block instantiation, linking, and memory allocation adds 100-300ms startup overhead. The pipeline is designed for long-running batch jobs where this amortizes over hours of processing.\n\n#### C. Configuration Constraints\n\n**Problem:** The `ProcessingOptions` class enforces a minimum `BatchWindowSeconds` of 60 seconds:\n```csharp\nif (BatchWindowSeconds is < 60 or > 3600)\n    throw new ConfigurationException(...);\n```\n\n**Impact:** This constraint was designed for hourly/daily batch semantics and doesn't accommodate the 30-60 second micro-batch cadence.\n\n#### D. S3 Write Pattern Mismatch\n\n**Problem:** The batch orchestrator writes results to S3 using a per-result pattern:\n```csharp\nawait PersistToLakeAsync(result, cancellationToken)\n```\n\n**Impact:** For micro-batches with 10-100 records, this creates significant I/O overhead. Each S3 PutObject has ~50-200ms latency, which can dominate processing time for small batches.\n\n#### E. Timer-Based Aggregation Assumptions\n\n**Problem:** Modules like `FocusTimeRatioTransformation` (module_43.txt) and `DeploymentLeadTimeStrategy` (module_10.txt) use `Timer` objects with 30-second to 5-minute flush intervals:\n```csharp\n_flushTimer = new Timer(_ => FlushBufferAsync(), null, _flushInterval, _flushInterval);\n```\n\n**Impact:** These timers assume long-running batch jobs. In a micro-batch scenario with Lambda invocations every 30-60 seconds, timers may not fire before the function terminates, or may misalign with batch boundaries.\n\n#### F. Full Batch Materialization\n\n**Problem:** The batch orchestrator materializes all requests to a list before processing:\n```csharp\nvar requestList = requests.ToList();\n```\n\n**Impact:** While acceptable for large batches where materialization cost is negligible, this adds unnecessary overhead for micro-batches.\n\n### 3. Architectural Solution Proposal\n\n#### Overview\n\nThe proposed solution creates a new **MicroBatchProcessor** that reuses the core transformation strategies from both pipelines while introducing a lightweight orchestration layer optimized for high-frequency, low-latency execution.\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    PROPOSED ARCHITECTURE                        │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                                 │\n│  ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────────────────────────┐     │\n│  │   Kinesis    │     │    MicroBatchTrigger (NEW)       │     │\n│  │   Stream     │────>│  - SQS/SNS/EventBridge based     │     │\n│  │              │     │  - Sub-minute polling            │     │\n│  └──────────────┘     │  - Warm Lambda via Provisioned   │     │\n│                       │    Concurrency                   │     │\n│                       └──────────────┬───────────────────┘     │\n│                                      │                         │\n│                                      ▼                         │\n│                       ┌──────────────────────────────────┐     │\n│                       │  MicroBatchOrchestrator (NEW)    │     │\n│                       │  - Singleton strategy registry   │     │\n│                       │  - Pre-warmed connections        │     │\n│                       │  - Batch-coalesced S3 writes     │     │\n│                       └──────────────┬───────────────────┘     │\n│                                      │                         │\n│            ┌─────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤\n│            │                         │                         │\n│            ▼                         ▼                         │\n│  ┌─────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────────┐         │\n│  │ REUSED: IKpi        │  │ REUSED: ITransformation │         │\n│  │ ComputationStrategy │  │ Strategy (module_39,    │         │\n│  │ (module_34, 60)     │  │ 80, 86)                 │         │\n│  │                     │  │                         │         │\n│  │ - FocusTimeRatio    │  │ - CalendarEventStrategy │         │\n│  │ - MeetingCostIndex  │  │ - DeploymentLeadTime    │         │\n│  │ - DeploymentLead    │  │ - GitPushStrategy       │         │\n│  └─────────────────────┘  └─────────────────────────┘         │\n│                                      │                         │\n│                                      ▼                         │\n│                       ┌──────────────────────────────────┐     │\n│                       │  BatchedS3Writer (NEW)           │     │\n│                       │  - Coalesces multiple results    │     │\n│                       │  - Single S3 multipart upload    │     │\n│                       │  - Optimized for small payloads  │     │\n│                       └──────────────────────────────────┘     │\n│                                                                 │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n#### New Components Required\n\n**1. MicroBatchTrigger** (new module)\n```csharp\n// Purpose: Lightweight Lambda trigger optimized for sub-minute invocations\npublic sealed class MicroBatchTrigger\n{\n    // Use SQS with batch window of 10 seconds and max batch size of 100\n    // Configure Lambda with 256MB memory and Provisioned Concurrency\n    private readonly MicroBatchOrchestrator _orchestrator;\n\n    public MicroBatchTrigger()\n    {\n        // Singleton orchestrator - warm across invocations\n        _orchestrator = MicroBatchOrchestratorSingleton.Instance;\n    }\n\n    public async Task HandleAsync(SQSEvent sqsEvent, ILambdaContext context)\n    {\n        // No assembly scanning - registry already warm\n        await _orchestrator.ProcessMicroBatchAsync(sqsEvent.Records, context.CancellationToken);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**2. MicroBatchOrchestrator** (new module)\n```csharp\n// Purpose: Orchestrator optimized for small batch sizes with pre-warmed dependencies\npublic sealed class MicroBatchOrchestrator\n{\n    // Singleton registry - no per-invocation discovery\n    private static readonly Lazy<TransformationStrategyRegistry> _registry =\n        new(() => new TransformationStrategyRegistry(), LazyThreadSafetyMode.ExecutionAndPublication);\n\n    // Pre-warmed S3 client\n    private static readonly IAmazonS3 _s3Client = new AmazonS3Client();\n\n    private readonly BatchedS3Writer _writer;\n\n    public MicroBatchOrchestrator()\n    {\n        _writer = new BatchedS3Writer(_s3Client);\n    }\n\n    public async Task ProcessMicroBatchAsync(IEnumerable<SQSMessage> messages, CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        var results = new List<KpiRecord>();\n\n        // Parallel processing with bounded concurrency\n        await Parallel.ForEachAsync(messages, new ParallelOptions\n        {\n            MaxDegreeOfParallelism = 4,  // Reduced for small batches\n            CancellationToken = ct\n        }, async (msg, token) =>\n        {\n            var strategy = _registry.Value.GetOrThrow(msg.EventType);\n            var result = await strategy.TransformAsync(msg.ToRawEvent(), token);\n            lock (results) results.AddRange(result);\n        });\n\n        // Single coalesced S3 write\n        await _writer.WriteBatchAsync(results, ct);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**3. BatchedS3Writer** (new module)\n```csharp\n// Purpose: Coalesce multiple small writes into single S3 operation\npublic sealed class BatchedS3Writer\n{\n    private readonly IAmazonS3 _s3;\n\n    public async Task WriteBatchAsync(IReadOnlyCollection<KpiRecord> records, CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        if (records.Count == 0) return;\n\n        // Single NDJSON file for entire micro-batch\n        var ndjson = string.Join('\\n', records.Select(r => JsonSerializer.Serialize(r)));\n        var key = $\"micro-batch/{DateTime.UtcNow:yyyy/MM/dd/HH}/{Guid.NewGuid()}.jsonl\";\n\n        await _s3.PutObjectAsync(new PutObjectRequest\n        {\n            BucketName = \"pulseops-data-lake\",\n            Key = key,\n            ContentBody = ndjson\n        }, ct);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Components Reused/Adapted\n\n| Component | Source Module | Adaptation |\n|-----------|--------------|------------|\n| `IKpiComputationStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_60.txt | Interface used as-is |\n| `FocusTimeRatioStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_60.txt, module_39.txt | Implementation used as-is |\n| `MeetingCostIndexStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_65.txt | Implementation used as-is |\n| `DeploymentLeadTimeStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_22.txt | Implementation used as-is |\n| `ITransformationStrategy` | module_39.txt, module_80.txt, module_86.txt | Interface used as-is |\n| Strategy implementations | module_35.txt, module_68.txt | Implementations used as-is |\n\n#### Configuration Changes\n\n```csharp\n// New MicroBatchProcessingOptions\npublic sealed record MicroBatchProcessingOptions\n{\n    public int ParallelShardCount { get; init; } = 2;  // Reduced parallelism\n    public int MaxBatchSize { get; init; } = 100;      // Small batch ceiling\n    public int TargetLatencyMs { get; init; } = 60000; // 60-second target\n    public bool UseProvisionedConcurrency { get; init; } = true;\n\n    public void Validate()\n    {\n        if (MaxBatchSize is < 1 or > 500)\n            throw new ConfigurationException(\"MaxBatchSize must be 1-500 for micro-batching.\");\n        if (TargetLatencyMs is < 10000 or > 90000)\n            throw new ConfigurationException(\"TargetLatencyMs must be 10-90 seconds for micro-batching.\");\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Justification: How This Overcomes Bottlenecks\n\n| Bottleneck | Solution | Benefit |\n|------------|----------|---------|\n| **High Startup Cost** (assembly scanning) | Singleton `Lazy<TransformationStrategyRegistry>` that persists across Lambda invocations | Amortizes ~1500ms cold-start across thousands of invocations |\n| **TPL Dataflow Overhead** | Direct `Parallel.ForEachAsync` without Dataflow infrastructure | Eliminates 100-300ms pipeline setup per invocation |\n| **Configuration Constraints** | New `MicroBatchProcessingOptions` with micro-batch-specific validation | Supports 30-60 second batch windows |\n| **S3 Write Pattern** | `BatchedS3Writer` coalesces all records into single NDJSON file | Reduces I/O from N×50ms to 1×50ms |\n| **Timer Misalignment** | No timers; synchronous batch completion | Ensures all records processed before Lambda terminates |\n| **Full Materialization** | Streaming `IEnumerable` processing where possible | Reduces memory allocation overhead |\n\n## Summary\n\n**Primary finding**: The existing batch processing pipeline is unsuitable for micro-batching due to high per-invocation initialization costs from reflection-based strategy discovery, TPL Dataflow setup overhead, and inefficient per-record S3 write patterns. These architectural decisions were appropriate for hourly/daily batch jobs processing millions of records but create unacceptable latency for 30-60 second micro-batches with a 90-second SLA.\n\n**Key components**:\n- **Stream pipeline**: module_72.txt (KinesisStreamProcessor), module_80.txt (StreamTransformationCoordinator), module_68.txt (TransformationLambda), module_22.txt (CalendarMetricLambda)\n- **Batch pipeline**: module_64.txt (KpiComputationOrchestrator), module_86.txt (TransformationOrchestrator), module_34.txt (KpiComputationEngine), module_35.txt (TransformationDispatcher), module_10.txt (KpiTransformationEngine), module_65.txt (EventTransformationPipeline)\n- **Configuration**: config.txt (ProcessingOptions with BatchWindowSeconds), constants.txt (resource identifiers)\n\n**Architectural pattern**: The proposed solution follows a **Singleton Warm Pool** pattern with **Batch Coalescing** - keeping expensive initialization costs as application-level singletons (rather than request-scoped) and coalescing multiple small I/O operations into single efficient operations.\n\n**Recommendations**:\n1. Create new `MicroBatchTrigger`, `MicroBatchOrchestrator`, and `BatchedS3Writer` modules\n2. Configure Lambda with Provisioned Concurrency to eliminate cold-starts\n3. Use SQS with batch window (10 seconds) as trigger instead of direct Kinesis\n4. Reuse all existing `IKpiComputationStrategy` and `ITransformationStrategy` implementations unchanged\n5. Add new `MicroBatchProcessingOptions` configuration class with appropriate validation bounds\n6. Core Components Identification\n\n**Stream Processing Pipeline:**\nThe stream processing pipeline is triggered by Kinesis Data Streams and designed for low-latency, per-record processing:\n\n| Module | Class | Purpose |\n|--------|-------|---------|\n| module_72.txt | `KinesisStreamProcessor` | Primary Lambda entry-point with lightweight cold-start |\n| module_80.txt | `StreamTransformationCoordinator` | High-throughput Kinesis processor with bounded parallelism |\n| module_68.txt | `TransformationLambda` | Kinesis-triggered Lambda with EventBridge fan-out |\n| module_22.txt | `CalendarMetricLambda` | Domain-specific stream processor |\n\n**Batch Processing Pipeline:**\nThe batch processing pipeline is designed for high-volume, scheduled runs (hourly/daily) with heavy initialization:\n\n| Module | Class | Purpose |\n|--------|-------|---------|\n| module_64.txt | `KpiComputationOrchestrator` | Main batch orchestrator with S3 persistence |\n| module_86.txt | `TransformationOrchestrator` | Glue/Step-Functions compatible orchestrator |\n| module_34.txt | `KpiComputationEngine` | Strategy-based KPI engine for batch jobs |\n| module_35.txt | `TransformationDispatcher` | Reflection-based strategy dispatcher |\n| module_10.txt | `KpiTransformationEngine` | Batch transformation with S3 parquet output |\n| module_65.txt | `EventTransformationPipeline` | TPL Dataflow-based batch pipeline |\n| module_60.txt | `KpiStrategyRegistry` | Attribute-based strategy discovery |\n\n### 2. Bottleneck Analysis for Micro-Batching\n\nThe current batch processing architecture has several critical bottlenecks that make it unsuitable for the 30-60 second micro-batch requirement with a 90-second SLA:\n\n#### A. High Startup/Cold-Start Overhead\n\n**Problem:** The batch modules rely on reflection-based strategy discovery that scans all loaded assemblies on every instantiation.\n\n```csharp\n// module_86.txt, module_60.txt, module_35.txt all use this pattern:\nvar strategies = AppDomain.CurrentDomain.GetAssemblies()\n    .SelectMany(a => a.GetTypes())\n    .Where(t => typeof(ITransformationStrategy).IsAssignableFrom(t))\n    .Select(Activator.CreateInstance)...\n```\n\n**Impact:** Assembly scanning and type activation takes 500ms-2000ms depending on deployment size. This alone could consume 2-3% of the 90-second SLA on every invocation.\n\n#### B. TPL Dataflow Pipeline Initialization\n\n**Problem:** The `EventTransformationPipeline` (module_65.txt) creates three TPL Dataflow blocks with bounded capacities and links them together on every instantiation:\n- `BufferBlock` with 1,024 capacity\n- `TransformManyBlock` with configurable parallelism\n- `ActionBlock` for sink operations\n\n**Impact:** Block instantiation, linking, and memory allocation adds 100-300ms startup overhead. The pipeline is designed for long-running batch jobs where this amortizes over hours of processing.\n\n#### C. Configuration Constraints\n\n**Problem:** The `ProcessingOptions` class enforces a minimum `BatchWindowSeconds` of 60 seconds:\n```csharp\nif (BatchWindowSeconds is < 60 or > 3600)\n    throw new ConfigurationException(...);\n```\n\n**Impact:** This constraint was designed for hourly/daily batch semantics and doesn't accommodate the 30-60 second micro-batch cadence.\n\n#### D. S3 Write Pattern Mismatch\n\n**Problem:** The batch orchestrator writes results to S3 using a per-result pattern:\n```csharp\nawait PersistToLakeAsync(result, cancellationToken)\n```\n\n**Impact:** For micro-batches with 10-100 records, this creates significant I/O overhead. Each S3 PutObject has ~50-200ms latency, which can dominate processing time for small batches.\n\n#### E. Timer-Based Aggregation Assumptions\n\n**Problem:** Modules like `FocusTimeRatioTransformation` (module_43.txt) and `DeploymentLeadTimeStrategy` (module_10.txt) use `Timer` objects with 30-second to 5-minute flush intervals:\n```csharp\n_flushTimer = new Timer(_ => FlushBufferAsync(), null, _flushInterval, _flushInterval);\n```\n\n**Impact:** These timers assume long-running batch jobs. In a micro-batch scenario with Lambda invocations every 30-60 seconds, timers may not fire before the function terminates, or may misalign with batch boundaries.\n\n#### F. Full Batch Materialization\n\n**Problem:** The batch orchestrator materializes all requests to a list before processing:\n```csharp\nvar requestList = requests.ToList();\n```\n\n**Impact:** While acceptable for large batches where materialization cost is negligible, this adds unnecessary overhead for micro-batches.\n\n### 3. Architectural Solution Proposal\n\n#### Overview\n\nThe proposed solution creates a new **MicroBatchProcessor** that reuses the core transformation strategies from both pipelines while introducing a lightweight orchestration layer optimized for high-frequency, low-latency execution.\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    PROPOSED ARCHITECTURE                        │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                                 │\n│  ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────────────────────────┐     │\n│  │   Kinesis    │     │    MicroBatchTrigger (NEW)       │     │\n│  │   Stream     │────>│  - SQS/SNS/EventBridge based     │     │\n│  │              │     │  - Sub-minute polling            │     │\n│  └──────────────┘     │  - Warm Lambda via Provisioned   │     │\n│                       │    Concurrency                   │     │\n│                       └──────────────┬───────────────────┘     │\n│                                      │                         │\n│                                      ▼                         │\n│                       ┌──────────────────────────────────┐     │\n│                       │  MicroBatchOrchestrator (NEW)    │     │\n│                       │  - Singleton strategy registry   │     │\n│                       │  - Pre-warmed connections        │     │\n│                       │  - Batch-coalesced S3 writes     │     │\n│                       └──────────────┬───────────────────┘     │\n│                                      │                         │\n│            ┌─────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤\n│            │                         │                         │\n│            ▼                         ▼                         │\n│  ┌─────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────────┐         │\n│  │ REUSED: IKpi        │  │ REUSED: ITransformation │         │\n│  │ ComputationStrategy │  │ Strategy (module_39,    │         │\n│  │ (module_34, 60)     │  │ 80, 86)                 │         │\n│  │                     │  │                         │         │\n│  │ - FocusTimeRatio    │  │ - CalendarEventStrategy │         │\n│  │ - MeetingCostIndex  │  │ - DeploymentLeadTime    │         │\n│  │ - DeploymentLead    │  │ - GitPushStrategy       │         │\n│  └─────────────────────┘  └─────────────────────────┘         │\n│                                      │                         │\n│                                      ▼                         │\n│                       ┌──────────────────────────────────┐     │\n│                       │  BatchedS3Writer (NEW)           │     │\n│                       │  - Coalesces multiple results    │     │\n│                       │  - Single S3 multipart upload    │     │\n│                       │  - Optimized for small payloads  │     │\n│                       └──────────────────────────────────┘     │\n│                                                                 │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n#### New Components Required\n\n**1. MicroBatchTrigger** (new module)\n```csharp\n// Purpose: Lightweight Lambda trigger optimized for sub-minute invocations\npublic sealed class MicroBatchTrigger\n{\n    // Use SQS with batch window of 10 seconds and max batch size of 100\n    // Configure Lambda with 256MB memory and Provisioned Concurrency\n    private readonly MicroBatchOrchestrator _orchestrator;\n\n    public MicroBatchTrigger()\n    {\n        // Singleton orchestrator - warm across invocations\n        _orchestrator = MicroBatchOrchestratorSingleton.Instance;\n    }\n\n    public async Task HandleAsync(SQSEvent sqsEvent, ILambdaContext context)\n    {\n        // No assembly scanning - registry already warm\n        await _orchestrator.ProcessMicroBatchAsync(sqsEvent.Records, context.CancellationToken);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**2. MicroBatchOrchestrator** (new module)\n```csharp\n// Purpose: Orchestrator optimized for small batch sizes with pre-warmed dependencies\npublic sealed class MicroBatchOrchestrator\n{\n    // Singleton registry - no per-invocation discovery\n    private static readonly Lazy<TransformationStrategyRegistry> _registry =\n        new(() => new TransformationStrategyRegistry(), LazyThreadSafetyMode.ExecutionAndPublication);\n\n    // Pre-warmed S3 client\n    private static readonly IAmazonS3 _s3Client = new AmazonS3Client();\n\n    private readonly BatchedS3Writer _writer;\n\n    public MicroBatchOrchestrator()\n    {\n        _writer = new BatchedS3Writer(_s3Client);\n    }\n\n    public async Task ProcessMicroBatchAsync(IEnumerable<SQSMessage> messages, CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        var results = new List<KpiRecord>();\n\n        // Parallel processing with bounded concurrency\n        await Parallel.ForEachAsync(messages, new ParallelOptions\n        {\n            MaxDegreeOfParallelism = 4,  // Reduced for small batches\n            CancellationToken = ct\n        }, async (msg, token) =>\n        {\n            var strategy = _registry.Value.GetOrThrow(msg.EventType);\n            var result = await strategy.TransformAsync(msg.ToRawEvent(), token);\n            lock (results) results.AddRange(result);\n        });\n\n        // Single coalesced S3 write\n        await _writer.WriteBatchAsync(results, ct);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**3. BatchedS3Writer** (new module)\n```csharp\n// Purpose: Coalesce multiple small writes into single S3 operation\npublic sealed class BatchedS3Writer\n{\n    private readonly IAmazonS3 _s3;\n\n    public async Task WriteBatchAsync(IReadOnlyCollection<KpiRecord> records, CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        if (records.Count == 0) return;\n\n        // Single NDJSON file for entire micro-batch\n        var ndjson = string.Join('\\n', records.Select(r => JsonSerializer.Serialize(r)));\n        var key = $\"micro-batch/{DateTime.UtcNow:yyyy/MM/dd/HH}/{Guid.NewGuid()}.jsonl\";\n\n        await _s3.PutObjectAsync(new PutObjectRequest\n        {\n            BucketName = \"pulseops-data-lake\",\n            Key = key,\n            ContentBody = ndjson\n        }, ct);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Components Reused/Adapted\n\n| Component | Source Module | Adaptation |\n|-----------|--------------|------------|\n| `IKpiComputationStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_60.txt | Interface used as-is |\n| `FocusTimeRatioStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_60.txt, module_39.txt | Implementation used as-is |\n| `MeetingCostIndexStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_65.txt | Implementation used as-is |\n| `DeploymentLeadTimeStrategy` | module_34.txt, module_22.txt | Implementation used as-is |\n| `ITransformationStrategy` | module_39.txt, module_80.txt, module_86.txt | Interface used as-is |\n| Strategy implementations | module_35.txt, module_68.txt | Implementations used as-is |\n\n#### Configuration Changes\n\n```csharp\n// New MicroBatchProcessingOptions\npublic sealed record MicroBatchProcessingOptions\n{\n    public int ParallelShardCount { get; init; } = 2;  // Reduced parallelism\n    public int MaxBatchSize { get; init; } = 100;      // Small batch ceiling\n    public int TargetLatencyMs { get; init; } = 60000; // 60-second target\n    public bool UseProvisionedConcurrency { get; init; } = true;\n\n    public void Validate()\n    {\n        if (MaxBatchSize is < 1 or > 500)\n            throw new ConfigurationException(\"MaxBatchSize must be 1-500 for micro-batching.\");\n        if (TargetLatencyMs is < 10000 or > 90000)\n            throw new ConfigurationException(\"TargetLatencyMs must be 10-90 seconds for micro-batching.\");\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Justification: How This Overcomes Bottlenecks\n\n| Bottleneck | Solution | Benefit |\n|------------|----------|---------|\n| **High Startup Cost** (assembly scanning) | Singleton `Lazy<TransformationStrategyRegistry>` that persists across Lambda invocations | Amortizes ~1500ms cold-start across thousands of invocations |\n| **TPL Dataflow Overhead** | Direct `Parallel.ForEachAsync` without Dataflow infrastructure | Eliminates 100-300ms pipeline setup per invocation |\n| **Configuration Constraints** | New `MicroBatchProcessingOptions` with micro-batch-specific validation | Supports 30-60 second batch windows |\n| **S3 Write Pattern** | `BatchedS3Writer` coalesces all records into single NDJSON file | Reduces I/O from N×50ms to 1×50ms |\n| **Timer Misalignment** | No timers; synchronous batch completion | Ensures all records processed before Lambda terminates |\n| **Full Materialization** | Streaming `IEnumerable` processing where possible | Reduces memory allocation overhead |\n\n## Summary\n\n**Primary finding**: The existing batch processing pipeline is unsuitable for micro-batching due to high per-invocation initialization costs from reflection-based strategy discovery, TPL Dataflow setup overhead, and inefficient per-record S3 write patterns. These architectural decisions were appropriate for hourly/daily batch jobs processing millions of records but create unacceptable latency for 30-60 second micro-batches with a 90-second SLA.\n\n**Key components**:\n- **Stream pipeline**: module_72.txt (KinesisStreamProcessor), module_80.txt (StreamTransformationCoordinator), module_68.txt (TransformationLambda), module_22.txt (CalendarMetricLambda)\n- **Batch pipeline**: module_64.txt (KpiComputationOrchestrator), module_86.txt (TransformationOrchestrator), module_34.txt (KpiComputationEngine), module_35.txt (TransformationDispatcher), module_10.txt (KpiTransformationEngine), module_65.txt (EventTransformationPipeline)\n- **Configuration**: config.txt (ProcessingOptions with BatchWindowSeconds), constants.txt (resource identifiers)\n\n**Architectural pattern**: The proposed solution follows a **Singleton Warm Pool** pattern with **Batch Coalescing** - keeping expensive initialization costs as application-level singletons (rather than request-scoped) and coalescing multiple small I/O operations into single efficient operations.\n\n**Recommendations**:\n1. Create new `MicroBatchTrigger`, `MicroBatchOrchestrator`, and `BatchedS3Writer` modules\n2. Configure Lambda with Provisioned Concurrency to eliminate cold-starts\n3. Use SQS with batch window (10 seconds) as trigger instead of direct Kinesis\n4. Reuse all existing `IKpiComputationStrategy` and `ITransformationStrategy` implementations unchanged\n5. Add new `MicroBatchProcessingOptions` configuration class with appropriate validation bounds\n6. Monitor P99 latency to ensure 90-second SLA compliance\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
